                                                                       Urals prices stabilize amid balanced sour complex

                                                                       CPC Blend differentials fall on weak demand

                                                                           The discount paid for Kazakh CPC Blend FOB Novorossiysk
                                                                       over Russian Urals fell to its lowest level in over six years on
                                                                       Oct. 23, as the Mediterranean sour complex remains balanced
                                                                       while sweet crude differentials experience a sharp correction.

would continue to influence trade, shipping and pricing decisions.                     for discounted Russian barrels.

    "For refiners in India and China, the waiver is really about
reducing the uncertainty rather than changing the crude supply
picture," said Premasish Das, executive director for oil analytics at
S&P Global Energy CERA. "Russian barrels were already flowing
into these markets, but the waiver removes an immediate concern
and gives refiners more confidence to continue buying grades that
make economic sense."

    "However, as the legislation remains in place, refiners may still
want to keep alternative supply options open in case the situation
changes," Das said.

    US President Donald Trump signed an executive order Oct. 16,
waiving measures included in the Lindsey O. Graham Sanctioning
Russia and Iran Act for the largest buyers of Russian oil, citing
"national interests," days before they were due to come into effect.

    The legislation targeted buyers of Russian hydrocarbons,
allowing the president to impose tariffs of up to 100% on goods
imported from the five largest buyers of Russian crude oil or natural
gas by total volume over the year before enactment, among other
measures.

Russian crude without immediately facing the threat of additional

    California's gasoline stockpiles rose during the week ended            imported gasoline at Brazilian ports by October. Most will already
Oct. 16, building on their recent recovery and pushing above the           have arrived, be berthed, or have discharged.

from Oct. 22.                                                              186,221 cubic meters, or 45% of October imports, underscoring

    Total gasoline inventories increased by 198,000 barrels
week over week to 10.28 million barrels, marking the second
consecutive week above the 10-million-barrel threshold. The
steady accumulation over recent weeks has significantly
improved the state's fuel cushion, lifting overall stockpiles above
the 9.95 million barrels recorded two years ago, though reserves
still trail the 11.38 million barrels held at this time last year.

    The weekly build was driven almost entirely by in-state
fuel supplies. Inventories of CARB reformulated gasoline
-- the backbone of California's retail market -- surged by
284,000 barrels to 4.87 million barrels, the highest level since
early August.

    The domestic accumulation comfortably offset minor
contractions across secondary categories, with blending
component reserves down 32,000 barrels to 4.68 million and out-
of-state non-California grades down 54,000 barrels to 731,000
barrels.

    Backwardation of the Asian naphtha market steepened amid
mounting tensions in the Middle East Oct. 23.

    Media reports citing US officials said plans had been prepared
for potential strikes on Iran. "Tankers have been attacked, there
have been reports of mines near the Strait of Hormuz, Saudi
facilities have been hit, embassy personnel have been moved and
the market is still digesting the possibility of renewed US-Israeli
strikes on Iran after the midterms," Quintex Global Strategist,
Stephen Innes, said in an Oct. 23 note.

    Meanwhile, Iran-backed Houthi militants claimed on X that the
group carried out four strikes against targets inside Saudi Arabia,
including a direct hit on Saudi Aramco facilities at Yanbu. Saudi
Aramco did not respond to a request for comment.

    In the derivatives market, brokers pegged the front-month
November-December Mean of Platts Japan swaps time spread at
$43/mt in midafternoon Asian trading Oct. 22, up $4.75/mt from
the previous close.

naphtha. South Korea's YNCC purchased open-spec naphtha with
a minimum of 70% paraffin content for first-half December delivery
to Yeosu, via a tender that closed Oct. 22, with same-day delivery.
